\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 8\delta+\\
&\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}}+c\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})}.\end{split}

(191)

where
\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}=\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}))^{\otimes\xi}
,
\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{L_{i}R_{i}}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\frac{k}{n}\xi}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}
, and

c=2((1+2\eta\max_{i}(\max(2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}},2^{H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})))^{N-1}-1).

(192)

By Eq. (
186
), we obtain that
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}=-\frac{k}{n}\xi
. Below, we analyze
H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}
. Note that for any
0<\delta<1
as long as
\xi\geq-\frac{8}{5}\log(1-\sqrt{1-\delta^2/N^2})
, we have

H(S|E)_{\rho}-\frac{4\log\gamma\sqrt{\log\frac{2N^{2}}{\delta^{2}}}}{\sqrt{\xi}}\leq\frac{1}{\xi}H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S|E)_{\rho^{\otimes\xi}}\leq H(S|E)_{\rho}+\frac{4\log\gamma\sqrt{\log\frac{2N^{2}}{\delta^{2}}}}{\sqrt{\xi}}.

(193)

with

\gamma\leq\sqrt{2^{-H^{\delta}_{\mathrm{min}}(S|E)_{\rho}}}+\sqrt{2^{H^{\delta}_{\mathrm{max}}(S|E)_{\rho}}}+1.

(194)

The condition
\xi\geq-\frac{8}{5}\log(1-\sqrt{1-\delta^2/N^2})
can be fulfilled as long as
n^{\frac{11}{8}}\epsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}
decays with
n
. In this case, given any
0<\delta<1
, for sufficiently large
n
, we have

2N^{2}(\frac{\epsilon}{n})^{\frac{5}{8}}\leq\delta^{2},

(195)

which implies

(1-(\frac{\epsilon}{n})^{\frac{5}{8}})^{2}\geq 1-\frac{\delta^{2}}{N^{2}},

(196)

and

\xi\geq-\frac{8}{5}\log(1-\sqrt{1-\delta^2/N^2}).

(197)

Note that based on the definition of the complementary channel
[
27
]
, given state
\outerproduct{\psi}{\psi}_{SS^{\prime}}
and the purification of
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\psi}{\psi}_{SS^{\prime}})
denoted as
\ket{\phi}_{SS^{\prime}E}
, we can get

\tr_{E}\outerproduct{\phi}{\phi}_{SS^{\prime}E}=\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}}(\outerproduct{\psi}{\psi}_{SS^{\prime}}).

(198)

Thus,

\begin{split}H(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\psi}{\psi}_{SS^{\prime}})}&=H(SE)-H(E)\\
&=H(S^{\prime})-H(SS^{\prime})\\
&=-H(S|S^{\prime})_{\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}}(\outerproduct{\psi}{\psi}_{SS^{\prime}})}.\end{split}

(199)

With direct calculation, we have that

\mathcal{N}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})=p_{I}\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}+p_{X}X\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}X+p_{Y}Y\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}Y+p_{Z}Z\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}Z,

(200)

and

\tr_{S}\mathcal{N}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})=\frac{\mathbb{I}}{2}.

(201)

Note that
\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}
,
X\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}X
,
Y\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}Y
, and
Z\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}Z
are mutually orthogonal. Hence,
H(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}=H(\tr_{S}\mathcal{N}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}}))-H(\mathcal{N}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}}))=1-h(\vec{p})
. Meanwhile, for any
0<\delta<1
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}4\log\gamma\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N^{2}}{\delta^{2}}}{\xi}}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}4\log\gamma\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N^{2}}{\delta^{2}}}{\log\frac{n}{\epsilon}}}=4\log\gamma\sqrt{2\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log n}{\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}}.

(202)

As long as
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log n}{\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}=0
, for sufficiently large
n
, we have that

4\log\gamma\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2N^{2}}{\delta^{2}}}{\xi}}\leq\delta.

(203)

This can be achieved by choosing
\log n/\varepsilon
as a function increasing slightly faster than
\log n
like
(\log n)\log\log\log n
. This additional factor
\log\log\log n
is normally negligible for a practical number
n
. Also, recall that by Eq. (
189
),
c\leq\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}
.
Thus, for sufficiently large
n
and any
0<\delta<1
, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}\xi(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)})}\\
=&8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-n(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)})}.\end{split}

(204)

When
1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta>0
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-n(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}.

(205)

Substituting the above equation into Eq. (
182
) completes the proof. The term
2^{-n(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}
is the error associated with the random Clifford encoding, as demonstrated in Appendix
B.12
.

As a remark, the decoupling error within Eq. (
205
) is dominated by
8\delta
. Thus, the scaling of
\delta
controls the decay rate of the decoupling error and the associated Choi error. From Eq. (
203
), we obtain that
\delta
can be chosen as the scaling of
O(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}})
. Thus, the scaling of the Choi error is
O((\frac{\log n}{\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}})^{\frac{1}{4}})
.

∎

B.5
Proof of Corollary
3
by applying non-smooth decoupling theorem to i.i.d. erasure error

Proof.

Following the previous derivation, the expectation of Choi error over
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
can be bounded by:

\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}}.

(206)

For i.i.d. erasure error, we have

\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{e})^{\otimes n}

(207)

with
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{e}
as the complementary channel of the single-qubit erasure error
\mathcal{N}_{e}(\rho)=(1-p)\rho+p\outerproduct{2}{2}
.

Applying the non-smooth decoupling theorem or Theorem
1
, the term in the square root in Eq. (
182
) satisfies

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}}+c\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})},\\
\end{split}

(208)

where
\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{e}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}))^{\otimes\xi}
,
\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\frac{k}{n}\xi}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}
, and

c=2((1+2\eta\max_{i}(\max(2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}},2^{H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})))^{N-1}-1).

(209)

Note that
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}=-\frac{k}{n}\xi
given by Eq. (
186
). Also, from Eq. (
187
) we can get

\begin{split}H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}&=\xi H_{2}(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{e}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{e}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{(1-p)\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}}+p\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2}\otimes\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}}}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{1-p}\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{I}_{S}\otimes\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\sqrt{1-p}\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S^{\prime}}}{2}+\sqrt{2p}\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}})^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}(\frac{1-p}{4}\mathbb{I}_{S^{\prime}}+2p\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}})\\
&=-\xi\log( \frac{1-p}{2} + 2p )\\
&=\xi(1-\log(1+3p)).\\
\end{split}

(210)

By Eq. (
189
),
c\leq\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}
.
Thus, for sufficiently large
n
, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}}+c\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-n(1-\log(1+3p)-\frac{k}{n})}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(1-\log(1+3p)-\frac{k}{n})})}.\end{split}

(211)

Substituting the above equation into Eq. (
206
) completes the proof. From the above equation, we have that as long as
\varepsilon
decays faster than
n^{-\frac{k}{n-k}}
, and encoding rate
k/n\leq 1-\log(1+3p)
, the Choi error decays polynomially. Hence, we conclude that 1D
O(\log(n))
-depth circuit achieves encoding rate
1-\log(1+3p)
for i.i.d. erasure error.
∎

B.6
Proof of Corollary
4
by applying smooth decoupling theorem to i.i.d. erasure error

Proof.

We follow the derivation in the last subsection but substitute the non-smooth decoupling theorem with the smooth decoupling theorem. Based on Eq. (
175
), we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 8\delta+\\
&\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}}+c\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})}.\end{split}

(212)

where
\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}=\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{e}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}))^{\otimes\xi}
,
\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{L_{i}R_{i}}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\frac{k}{n}\xi}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}
, and

c=2((1+2\eta\max_{i}(\max(2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}},2^{H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})))^{N-1}-1).

(213)

Note that
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}=-\frac{k}{n}\xi
given by Eq. (
186
). Same with Appendix
B.4
, as long as
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log n}{\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}=0
, for sufficiently large
n
, we have that

\absolutevalue{\frac{1}{\xi}H^{\delta/N}_{2}(S|E)_{\rho^{\otimes\xi}} - H(S|E)_{\rho}}\leq\delta.

(214)

Meanwhile, from Eq. (
199
) we have

\begin{split}H(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}&=\xi H(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{e}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}\\
&=-\xi H(S|S^{\prime})_{\mathcal{N}_{e}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}\\
&=-\xi H(S|S^{\prime})_{(1-p)\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}}+p\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2}\otimes\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}}}\\
&=\xi(H(S^{\prime})_{\frac{1-p}{2}\mathbb{I}_{S^{\prime}}+p\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}}}-H(SS^{\prime})_{(1-p)\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}}+p\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2}\otimes\outerproduct{2}{2}_{S^{\prime}}})\\
&=\xi(-p\log p-(1-p)\log\frac{1-p}{2}+(1-p)\log(1-p)+p\log\frac{p}{2})\\
&=\xi(1-2p).\\
\end{split}

(215)

Also, by Eq. (
189
),
c\leq\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}
. Thus, for sufficiently large
n
and
0<\delta<1
, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}\xi(1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)})}\\
=&8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-n(1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)})}.\end{split}

(216)

When
1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta>0
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-n(1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}.

(217)

Substituting the above equation into Eq. (
206
) completes the proof. From the above equation, we have that the encoding rate
k/n
can achieve
1-2p
for i.i.d. erasure error with 1D
\omega(\log(n))
-depth circuit. This encoding rate is also the quantum channel capacity of the erasure error.
∎

B.7
Proof of Corollary
5
by applying non-smooth decoupling theorem to local amplitude damping noise

Proof.

Following the previous derivation for local Pauli noise and erasure errors, the key point is to evaluate
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}
and
H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}
. Similar to previous analysis,
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}=-\frac{k}{n}\xi
.

For local amplitude damping noise, we have

\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{a})^{\otimes n},

(218)

with
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
as the complementary channel of the amplitude damping noise. Thus,

\begin{split}H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{a}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(\ket{00}+\sqrt{1-p}\ket{11})(\bra{00}+\sqrt{1-p}\bra{11})_{SS^{\prime}}+\frac{p}{2}\outerproduct{10}{10}_{SS^{\prime}}}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{\frac{2-p}{2}(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2-p}}\ket{00}+\sqrt{\frac{1-p}{2-p}}\ket{11})(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2-p}}\bra{00}+\sqrt{\frac{1-p}{2-p}}\bra{11})_{SS^{\prime}}+\frac{p}{2}\outerproduct{10}{10}_{SS^{\prime}}}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\tr_{S}(\sqrt{\frac{2-p}{2}}(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2-p}}\ket{00}+\sqrt{\frac{1-p}{2-p}}\ket{11})(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2-p}}\bra{00}+\sqrt{\frac{1-p}{2-p}}\bra{11})_{SS^{\prime}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{2}}\outerproduct{10}{10}_{SS^{\prime}}))^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}(((\frac{1}{\sqrt{4-2p}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{2}})\outerproduct{0}{0}_{S^{\prime}}+\frac{1-p}{\sqrt{4-2p}}\outerproduct{1}{1}_{S^{\prime}})^{2})\\
&=-\xi\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}((\frac{1}{\sqrt{4-2p}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{2}})^{2}\outerproduct{0}{0}_{S^{\prime}}+\frac{(1-p)^{2}}{4-2p}\outerproduct{1}{1}_{S^{\prime}})\\
&=-\xi\log( (\frac{1}{\sqrt{4-2p}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{2}})^2 + \frac{(1-p)^2}{4-2p} )\\
&=-\xi\log( \frac{1}{2-p} + \sqrt{\frac{p}{2-p}} ).\\
\end{split}

(219)

By Eq. (
189
),
c\leq\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}
.
Thus, for sufficiently large
n
, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2N}H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}}+c\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-n(-\log( \frac{1}{2-p} + \sqrt{\frac{p}{2-p}} )-\frac{k}{n})}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(-\log( \frac{1}{2-p} + \sqrt{\frac{p}{2-p}}-\frac{k}{n})})}.\end{split}

(220)

From the above equation, we have that as long as
\varepsilon
decays faster than
n^{-\frac{k}{n-k}}
, and encoding rate
k/n\leq-\log( \frac{1}{2-p} + \sqrt{\frac{p}{2-p}} )
, the Choi error decays polynomially. Hence, we conclude that 1D
O(\log(n))
-depth circuit achieves encoding rate
-\log( \frac{1}{2-p} + \sqrt{\frac{p}{2-p}} )
for local amplitude damping noise.
∎

B.8
Proof of Corollary
6
by applying smooth decoupling theorem to local amplitude damping noise

Proof.

Following the previous derivation, the key point is to evaluate
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}
and
H(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}
. Similar to previous analysis,
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}=-\frac{k}{n}\xi
. Meanwhile, from Eq. (
199
) we have

\begin{split}H(S_{i}|E_{i})_{\tau_{S_{i}E_{i}}}&=-\xi H(S|S^{\prime})_{\mathcal{N}_{a}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}\\
&=-\xi H(S|S^{\prime})_{\frac{1}{2}(\ket{00}+\sqrt{1-p}\ket{11})(\bra{00}+\sqrt{1-p}\bra{11})_{SS^{\prime}}+\frac{p}{2}\outerproduct{10}{10}_{SS^{\prime}}}\\
&=\xi(H(S^{\prime})_{\frac{1+p}{2}\outerproduct{0}{0}_{S^{\prime}}+\frac{1-p}{2}\outerproduct{1}{1}_{S^{\prime}}}-H(SS^{\prime})_{\frac{1}{2}(\ket{00}+\sqrt{1-p}\ket{11})(\bra{00}+\sqrt{1-p}\bra{11})_{SS^{\prime}}+\frac{p}{2}\outerproduct{10}{10}_{SS^{\prime}}})\\
&=\xi(h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})).\\
\end{split}

(221)

Also, by Eq. (
189
),
c\leq\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}
. Thus, for sufficiently large
n
and
0<\delta<1
, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-n(h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)})}.\end{split}

(222)

When
h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta>0
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 8\delta+\sqrt{2^{-n(h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}.

(223)

From the above equation, we have that the encoding rate
k/n
can achieve
h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})
for local amplitude damping noise with 1D
\omega(\log(n))
-depth circuit, which is the same as the bound with random stabilizer codes.
∎

B.9
Proof of Corollary
7
by applying the non-smooth decoupling theorem to nearest-neighbour
ZZ
-coupling noise

Proof.

Following the previous derivation, the expectation of Choi error over
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
can be bounded by:

\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}}.

(224)

For 1D nearest neighbor
ZZ
-coupling noise, we have

\mathcal{N}_{S}=\mathcal{N}_{zz}=\circ_{i=1}^{n-1}((1-p)\mathcal{I}+p\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i+1}).

(225)

Applying the non-smooth decoupling theorem or Theorem
1
, the term in the square root in Eq. (
182
) satisfies

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-H_{2}(S|E)_{\tau_{SE}}-H_{2}(S|R)_{\rho_{SR}}}+c\max_{A\subseteq[2N]}2^{-H_{2}(A|R)_{\rho_{AR}}-H_{2}(A|E)_{\tau_{AE}}}},\end{split}

(226)

where
\tau_{SE}=\mathcal{N}_{zz}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\otimes n})
,
\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\frac{k}{n}\xi}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}{0^{\frac{n-k}{n}\xi}}
,
\tau_{AE}=\tr_{\bar{A}}\tau_{SE}
,
\rho_{SR}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2N}\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}
, and

c=2((1+2\eta\max_{i}(\max(2^{-H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}},2^{H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}})))^{N-1}-1).

(227)

Note that
H_{2}(S_{i}|R_{i})_{\rho_{S_{i}R_{i}}}=-\frac{k}{n}\xi
given by Eq. (
186
). Thus,
H_{2}(A|R)_{\rho_{AR}}=-\frac{k\absolutevalue{A}}{n}\xi
.

Also, from Eq. (
187
) we can get

\begin{split}H_{2}(A|E)_{\tau_{AE}}&=-\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}[\tr_{A}\sqrt{\tr_{\bar{A}}\mathcal{N}_{zz}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\otimes n})}]\\
&=-\log\tr_{A^{\prime}}[\tr_{A}\sqrt{\prod_{i:i\in S_{A},i+1\in S_{\bar{A}}}[(1-p)\mathcal{I}+p\mathcal{Z}_{i}]\prod_{\{i,i+1\}\subseteq S_{A}}[(1-p)\mathcal{I}+p\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i+1}](\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{AA^{\prime}})}]\\
&=\absolutevalue{A}\xi-2\log( \sum_{\vec{Z}\in\{\mathcal{I},\mathcal{Z}\}^{\otimes\abs{A}\xi}}\Pr(\vec{Z}) )\\
&\geq\absolutevalue{A}\xi-2\log( (\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})^{\abs{A}(\xi+1)})\\
&=\xi\absolutevalue{A}[1-2(1+\xi^{-1})\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})]\\
&\approx\xi\absolutevalue{A}[1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})].\end{split}

(228)

Here,
\Pr(\vec{Z})
is the probability of pattern
\vec{Z}
generated by
\prod_{i:i\in S_{A},i+1\in S_{\bar{A}}}[(1-p)\mathcal{I}+p\mathcal{Z}_{i}]\prod_{\{i,i+1\}\subseteq S_{A}}[(1-p)\mathcal{I}+p\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i+1}]
. Note that this probability can be obtained by coarse-graining the binomial distribution
B(\absolutevalue{A}(\xi+1),p)
. This can be seen by counting the generators of this probability distribution:

\absolutevalue{A}(\xi+1)\geq\absolutevalue{\{ i | i\in S_A, i+1\in S_{\Bar{A}} \}}+\absolutevalue{ \{ i| \{i,i+1\}\subseteq S_A \} }.

(229)

The coarse-graining operation can only decrease
\sum\Pr(\vec{Z})
, which gives us the inequality in the fourth line.

By Eq. (
189
),
c\leq\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}
.
Thus, for sufficiently large
n
, we have
\xi^{-1}\approx 0
and obtain that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-H_{2}(S|E)_{\tau_{SE}}-H_{2}(S|R)_{\rho_{SR}}}+c\max_{A\subseteq[2N]}2^{-H_{2}(A|R)_{\rho_{AR}}-H_{2}(A|E)_{\tau_{AE}}}}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-n(1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})-\frac{k}{n})}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}\max(1,2^{-\xi(1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})-\frac{k}{n})})}.\end{split}

(230)

Substituting the above equation into Eq. (
224
) completes the proof. From the above equation, we have that as long as
\varepsilon
decays faster than
n^{-\frac{k}{n-k}}
, and encoding rate
k/n<1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})
, the Choi error decays polynomially. Hence, we conclude that a 1D
O(\log(n))
-depth circuit achieves an encoding rate
1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})
for nearest neighbor
ZZ
-coupling noise, which is the same with that of random Clifford encoding scheme, manifested by Eq. (
300
).
∎

B.10
Approximate quantum error correction against the fixed-number random erasure errors for
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}

We first present the results of AQEC performance of
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
for random
t
-erasure error below. Then we give the full proof. The proof idea is similar to that of the non-decoupling theorem.

Theorem 5

(AQEC performance of
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
for random
t
-erasure error)
.

In the large
n
limit, suppose
k
and
t
satisfy
1-\frac{k}{n}-\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})\geq 0
, and
\varepsilon
satisfies that
(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{1-\frac{k}{n}}=o(\frac{1}{n})
, then the expected Choi error of the random codes from
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
against the random
t
erasure errors satisfies

\mathbb{E}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\left(2^{-(n-2t-k)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(231)

Proof.

Here, we analyze the random erasure error with a fixed number of erasures. We utilize the complementary channel to analyze the Choi error. Specifically,

\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}=\min_{\zeta}P\left((\widehat{\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{E}}_{L\rightarrow E}\otimes I_{R})(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}),(\mathcal{T}^{\zeta}_{L\rightarrow E}\otimes I_{R})(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR})\right).

(232)

Since
\mathcal{T}^{\zeta}_{L\rightarrow E}
maps all states to a fixed state
\zeta
,
(\mathcal{T}^{\zeta}_{L\rightarrow E}\otimes I_{R})(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR})=\zeta_{E}\otimes\tr_{L}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR})=\zeta_{E}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}
. Meanwhile,
\widehat{\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{E}}_{L\rightarrow E}=\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}\circ\mathcal{E}_{L\rightarrow S}
with
\mathcal{E}_{L\rightarrow S}
the encoding map satisfying

\mathcal{E}_{L\rightarrow S}(\rho)=U_{S}(\rho_{L}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}^{n-k})U_{S}^{\dagger},

(233)

where
U_{S}
is the encoding unitary, generated by 1D
\log(n/\varepsilon)
-depth Clifford circuits in our case. The term
\outerproduct{0}{0}^{n-k}
is the ancillary qubits used for quantum error correction. For a fixed
t
-erasure errors,
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\rho)=\tr_{n-t}(\rho)
. For a random
t
-erasure error, which is a mixing of
t
-erasure errors at different locations,
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\rho)=\frac{1}{\binom{n}{t}}\sum_{\absolutevalue{T}=t}\outerproduct{T}{T}\otimes\tr_{S\backslash T}(\rho)

[
72
]
where
\ket{T}
labels the location of the erasure errors.
Hence,

\begin{split}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}=&\min_{\zeta}P\left(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}),\zeta_{E}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\right)\\
\leq&P\left(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}),\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{U}_{n}}\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})\right)\\
&+\min_{\zeta}P\left(\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{U}_{n}}\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}),\zeta_{E}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\right)\\
=&P\left(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}),\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\right)+\min_{\zeta}P\left(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}},\zeta_{E}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\right)\\
=&P\left(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}),\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\right).\end{split}

(234)

Here, in the first inequality, we utilize the triangle inequality of the purified distance. In the second equality, we utilize the property of the twirling over Haar random unitary gates. In the third equality, we take the state
\zeta
as
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})
.

In our case, the encoding unitary is generated by random 1D
\log(n/\varepsilon)
-depth Clifford circuits with the ensemble denoted as
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
. Then, we aim to bound
\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}
. For simplicity, we omit
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
in the subscript. Utilizing Eq. (
15
) we get

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq&\mathbb{E}_{U}\sqrt{\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}}\\
\leq&\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}}.\end{split}

(235)

When the complementary channel is
\mathbb{E}_{i}\outerproduct{i}{i}\otimes\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{i}_{S\rightarrow E}
where
\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{i}_{S\rightarrow E}
is the complementary channel of
\mathcal{N}^{i}_{S\rightarrow E}
, we have that

\begin{split}&\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
=&\mathbb{E}_{i}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{i}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{i}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1},\end{split}

(236)

due to that
\|\mathbb{E}_{i}\outerproduct{i}{i}\otimes A_{i}\|_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{i}\|A_{i}\|_{1}
. Below, we use
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}
to represent a case of mixing noise channels. For the mixing noise channel, we have

\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{U}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}}.

(237)

For further evaluation, we use
\Phi_{Haar}
to denote
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}
. Using
\|A\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\absolutevalue{A}}\|A\|_{2}
, we further get

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq&\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{U}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\Phi_{Haar}\|_{1}}\\
\leq&\sqrt{\sqrt{\dim E\dim R}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\Phi_{Haar}\|_{2}}\\
\leq&(\dim E\dim R)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\mathbb{E}_{U}\tr(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\ketbra{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\ketbra{0^{n-k}} U^{\dagger}) - \Phi_{Haar} )^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
=&(\dim E\dim R)^{\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}+\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}\\
&-2\tr\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\mathbb{E}_{U}[U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}])\Phi_{Haar}])^{\frac{1}{4}}.\\
\end{split}

(238)

In the last line, we expand the 2-norm. The error terms can be divided into two parts:

\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2},

(239)

and

\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}-2\tr\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\mathbb{E}_{U}[U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}])\Phi_{Haar}.

(240)

Eqs. (
239
) and (
240
) can be interpreted as the error brought by the second and the first moments of random unitary operations, respectively. Provided that
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
forms a unitary 1-design, we have

\mathbb{E}_{U}[U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}]=\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}.

(241)

Thus,

\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}-2\tr\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\mathbb{E}_{U}[U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}])\Phi_{Haar}=-\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar},

(242)

where

\begin{split}\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}&=\tr([\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\id_S}{2^n})]^2\otimes\frac{\id_R}{2^{2k}})\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\tr([\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\id_S}{2^n})]^2).\end{split}

(243)

For the erasure channel,
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}=\tr_{n-t}
,
\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}=\frac{1}{2^{k+t}}=\frac{1}{\dim E\dim R}
, and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}=\frac{1}{2^{k+t}}
.

The remained part to bound
\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}
is
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}
. We first evaluate
\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}
and then take the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}
. We use
\tr A^{2}=\tr A^{\otimes 2}F
to evaluate it with
F
being a SWAP operator. Hence,

\begin{split}&\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}\\
=&\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger}))^{\otimes 2}]F_{E}\otimes F_{R}\\
=&\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[U^{\otimes 2}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2}U^{\dagger\otimes 2}](\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_{E})\otimes F_{R},\end{split}

(244)

where
F_{E}
denotes the SWAP operator acting on two copies of system
E
, and
F_{R}
is defined similarly. The term
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger}
is the adjoint map of
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}
. To further evaluate the above quantity, we need to investigate the structure of
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
. Note that the random encoding unitary
U
can be generated by two layers, as shown in Fig.
1
. The qubits are divided into
2N
parts, labelled from
1
to
2N
, with each part having an equal size of
\xi=\frac{n}{2N}
qubits. The first layer of the encoding unitary is a tensor product of random Clifford gates acting on the
2i-1
and
2i
parts, and the second layer is a tensor product of random Clifford gates acting on the
2i
and
2i+1
parts. That is,

U=U_{2}\cdot U_{1},

(245)

where

U_{1}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}U_{C}^{2i-1,2i},U_{2}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N-1}U_{C}^{2i,2i+1}.

(246)

The gate
U_{C}^{i,i+1}
is uniformly and randomly sampled from the Clifford group in the regions
i
and
i+1
. As a result,

\begin{split}&\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}\\
=&\tr\mathbb{E}_{U_{1}}[U_{1}^{\otimes 2}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2}U_{1}^{\dagger\otimes 2}]\mathbb{E}_{U_{2}}[U_{2}^{\dagger\otimes 2}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_{E})U_{2}^{\otimes 2}]\otimes F_{R}\\
=&\tr( \tr_R(\mathbb{E}_{U_1}[U_1^{\otimes 2}(\ketbra{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\ketbra{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2} U_1^{\dagger\otimes 2}]F_R) \mathbb{E}_{U_2}[U_2^{\dagger\otimes 2}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_E)U_2^{\otimes 2}]).\end{split}

(247)

We make the two layers of random unitary gates act on different terms for further evaluation. We first focus on the first term,

\tr_{R}(\mathbb{E}_{U_{1}}[U_{1}^{\otimes 2}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2}U_{1}^{\dagger\otimes 2}]F_{R}).

(248)

For simplicity, we consider each part to have the same number of encoding qubits and ancillary qubits. That is, in part
i
, there will be
\frac{k}{n}\xi=\frac{k}{2N}
encoding qubits and
\frac{n-k}{n}\xi=\frac{n-k}{2N}
ancillary qubits. Thus, based on Eqs. (
51
) and (
246
), we get

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U_{1}}[U_{1}^{\otimes 2}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2}U_{1}^{\dagger\otimes 2}]\\
=&\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{R_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d^{2}_{R_{2i-1,2i}}}}+\frac{F_{S_{2i-1,2i}}-\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}}{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}-1}\otimes(F_{R_{2i-1,2i}}-\frac{1}{d_{S_{2i-1,2i}}})\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{R_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d^{2}_{R_{2i-1,2i}}}}\right).\end{split}

(249)

Since
F_{R}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}F_{R_{2i-1,2i}}
, we have

\begin{split}&\tr_{R}(\mathbb{E}_{U_{1}}[U_{1}^{\otimes 2}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2}U_{1}^{\dagger\otimes 2}]F_{R})\\
=&\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}d_{R_{2i-1,2i}}}}+\frac{F_{S_{2i-1,2i}}-\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}}{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}-1}(1-\frac{1}{d_{S_{2i-1,2i}}d_{R_{2i-1,2i}}})\right)\\
=&\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d^{4}_{S_{i}}d^{2}_{R_{i}}}}+\frac{F_{S_{2i-1,2i}}-\frac{\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1,2i}}}}{{d^{2}_{S_{i}}}}}{d^{4}_{S_{i}}-1}(1-\frac{1}{d^{2}_{S_{i}}d^{2}_{R_{i}}})\right)\\
=&\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}(a\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i-1}}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{2i}}}+bF_{S_{2i-1}}\otimes F_{S_{2i}})\\
=&\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}(a\mathbb{I}_{2i-1}\mathbb{I}_{2i}+bF_{2i-1}F_{2i}).\end{split}

(250)

Here, the last line is a simple notation of the third line. That is, we use
\mathbb{I}_{i}
and
F_{i}
to denote
\mathbb{I}_{d^{2}_{S_{i}}}
and
F_{S_{i}}
, respectively, and we omit the tensor product;
d_{S_{i}}=2^{\xi}
represents the dimension of part
i
and correspondingly,
d_{R_{i}}=2^{\frac{k}{n}\xi}
. The coefficients

\begin{split}a&=\frac{1}{{d^{4}_{S_{i}}d^{2}_{R_{i}}}}-\frac{1}{d^{2}_{S_{i}}(d^{4}_{S_{i}}-1)}(1-\frac{1}{d^{2}_{S_{i}}d^{2}_{R_{i}}})\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{4\xi+\frac{2k}{n}\xi}}-\frac{1}{2^{2\xi}(2^{4\xi}-1)}(1-\frac{1}{2^{2\xi+\frac{2k}{n}\xi}}),\end{split}

(251)

and

\begin{split}b&=\frac{1}{d^{4}_{S_{i}}-1}(1-\frac{1}{d^{2}_{S_{i}}d^{2}_{R_{i}}})\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{4\xi}-1}(1-\frac{1}{2^{2\xi+\frac{2k}{n}\xi}}).\end{split}

(252)

It is easy to see that
a\leq 2^{-4\xi-\frac{2k}{n}\xi}
and
b\leq 2^{-4\xi}
.

Next, we consider the second term
\mathbb{E}_{U_{2}}[U_{2}^{\dagger\otimes 2}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_{E})U_{2}^{\otimes 2}]
with
\mathcal{N}
a fixed
t
-erasure channel. Note that
(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_{E})=F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}}
, where
\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}}
denotes the identity operator on two copies of subsystem
\bar{E}
.
Combined with Eqs. (
51
) and (
246
), we have

\mathbb{E}_{U_{2}}[U_{2}^{\dagger\otimes 2}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_{E})U_{2}^{\otimes 2}]=(F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}})_{1}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N-1}(a_{2i,2i+1}\mathbb{I}_{2i}\mathbb{I}_{2i+1}+b_{2i,2i+1}F_{2i}F_{2i+1})(F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}})_{2N},

(253)

where

a_{2i,2i+1}=\frac{2^{n^{2i}_{I}+n^{2i+1}_{I}}}{2^{2\xi}}\frac{2^{4\xi}-2^{2n^{2i}_{F}+2n^{2i+1}_{F}}}{2^{4\xi}-1},b_{2i,2i+1}=\frac{2^{n^{2i}_{I}+n^{2i+1}_{I}}(2^{2n^{2i}_{F}+2n^{2i+1}_{F}}-1)}{2^{4\xi}-1}.

(254)

Here,
(F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}})_{i}
denotes the restriction of
F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}}
is part
i
. Note that
(F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}})_{i}
is a tensor product of identity operators and SWAP operators. The terms
n^{i}_{I}
and
n^{i}_{F}
denote the number of identity operators and SWAP operators in
(F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}})_{i}
, respectively. Hence,
n^{i}_{I}+n^{i}_{F}=\xi
and
\sum_{i}n^{i}_{F}=t
.

Combined the results above, we get

\begin{split}&\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}\\
=&\tr( \tr_R(\mathbb{E}_{U_1}[U_1^{\otimes 2}(\ketbra{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\ketbra{0^{n-k}})^{\otimes 2} U_1^{\dagger\otimes 2}]F_R) \mathbb{E}_{U_2}[U_2^{\dagger\otimes 2}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}^{\dagger})^{\otimes 2}(F_E)U_2^{\otimes 2}])\\
=&\tr(\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^N (a\id_{2i-1}\id_{2i}+bF_{2i-1}F_{2i})\right) \left( (F_E\otimes\id_{\Bar{E}})_1 \bigotimes_{i=1}^{N-1} (a_{2i, 2i+1}\id_{2i}\id_{2i+1}+b_{2i,2i+1}F_{2i}F_{2i+1})(F_E\otimes\id_{\Bar{E}})_{2N}\right))\\
=&\begin{pmatrix}2^{2n^{2N}_{I}+n^{2N}_{F}}&2^{n^{2N}_{I}+2n^{2N}_{F}}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&b\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}2^{2\xi}&2^{\xi}\\
2^{\xi}&2^{2\xi}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a_{2i,2i+1}&0\\
0&b_{2i,2i+1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}2^{2\xi}&2^{\xi}\\
2^{\xi}&2^{2\xi}\end{pmatrix}\right)\begin{pmatrix}a2^{2n^{1}_{I}+n^{1}_{F}}\\
b2^{n^{1}_{I}+2n^{1}_{F}}\end{pmatrix}\\
=&2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}2^{n^{2N}_{I}}&2^{n^{2N}_{F}}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&b\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2i}u_{2i+1}&\eta(u_{2i}u_{2i+1}+v_{2i}v_{2i+1})\\
\eta(u_{2i}u_{2i+1}+v_{2i}v_{2i+1})&v_{2i}v_{2i+1}\end{pmatrix}\right)\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&b\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}2^{n^{1}_{I}}\\
2^{n^{1}_{F}}\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

(255)

Here, the third line is a direct substitution. The fourth line is done by direct calculation. Note that

\tr_{1}((a\mathbb{I}_{1}\mathbb{I}_{2}+bF_{1}F_{2})(F_{E}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\bar{E}})_{1})=(a2^{2n^{1}_{I}+n^{1}_{F}}\mathbb{I}_{2}+b2^{n^{1}_{I}+2n^{1}_{F}}F_{2}),

(256)

and

\tr_{1}((x\mathbb{I}_{1}+yF_{1})(a\mathbb{I}_{1}\mathbb{I}_{2}+bF_{1}F_{2}))=(a2^{2\xi}x+a2^{\xi}y)\mathbb{I}_{2}+(b2^{\xi}x+b2^{2\xi}y)F_{2}.

(257)

By using the above two equalities, we derive the fourth line. For the fifth line, we use that
n^{i}_{I}+n^{i}_{F}=\xi
and
2N\xi=n
. Also, we calculate

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}2^{\xi}&1\\
1&2^{\xi}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a_{2i,2i+1}&0\\
0&b_{2i,2i+1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}2^{\xi}&1\\
1&2^{\xi}\end{pmatrix}&=\begin{pmatrix}2^{2\xi}a_{2i,2i+1}+b_{2i,2i+1}&2^{\xi}(a_{2i,2i+1}+b_{2i,2i+1})\\
2^{\xi}(a_{2i,2i+1}+b_{2i,2i+1})&a_{2i,2i+1}+2^{2\xi}b_{2i,2i+1}\end{pmatrix}\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}u_{2i}u_{2i+1}&\eta(u_{2i}u_{2i+1}+v_{2i}v_{2i+1})\\
\eta(u_{2i}u_{2i+1}+v_{2i}v_{2i+1})&v_{2i}v_{2i+1}\end{pmatrix},\end{split}

(258)

where

u_{i}=2^{n^{i}_{I}},v_{i}=2^{n^{i}_{F}},\eta=\frac{2^{\xi}}{2^{2\xi}+1}\leq 2^{-\xi}.

(259)

Denote
D_{2i,2i+1}=\begin{pmatrix}u_{2i}u_{2i+1}&0\\
0&v_{2i}v_{2i+1}\end{pmatrix}
,
E_{2i,2i+1}=\eta(u_{2i}u_{2i+1}+v_{2i}v_{2i+1})\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}
, and
C=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&b\end{pmatrix}
, we have

\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}=2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2N}&v_{2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}C(D_{2i,2i+1}+E_{2i,2i+1})\right)C\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{pmatrix}.

(260)

This equation is similar to
(D+E)^{N-1}
, which enables us to expand it according to the number of “
E
.” We first consider the expansion term with no
E
. That is,

\begin{split}2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2N}&v_{2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)C\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{pmatrix}&=2^{n}(a^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}u_{i}+b^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{2N}v_{i})\\
&=2^{n}(a^{N}2^{n-t}+b^{N}2^{t})\\
&\leq 2^{n}(2^{-2n-k}2^{n-t}+2^{-2n}2^{t})\\
&=2^{-t-k}+2^{-n+t}\\
\end{split}

(261)

The first term
2^{-t-k}
in this equation equals
\tr\Phi^{2}_{Haar}
and will be eliminated in the final result. The second term
2^{-n+t}
exponentially decays with respect to
n
. Combining with the coefficient
\dim E\dim R=2^{t+k}
, this is
2^{-(n-2t-k)}
corresponding to a result of random Clifford encoding, as demonstrated in Appendix
B.12
. In fact, if the encoding unitary is drawn from the whole unitary group instead of
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
,
\eta
will quickly decay to 0, and it is unnecessary to consider
E
in the expansion. The expansion terms with
E
can be viewed as extra errors.

Below, we consider the case there are
g
numbers of
E
in the expansion. We set the location of
E
as
j_{1},j_{2},\cdots,j_{g}
with
1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<\cdots<j_{g}\leq N-1
. That is,

\begin{split}&2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2N}&v_{2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=j_{g}+1}^{N-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)CE_{2j_{g},2j_{g}+1}\cdots CE_{2j_{2},2j_{2}+1}\left(\prod_{i=j_{1}+1}^{j_{2}-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)CE_{2j_{1},2j_{1}+1}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j_{1}-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)C\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{pmatrix}\\
=&2^{n}\eta^{g}\prod_{r=1}^{g}(u_{2j_{r}}u_{2j_{r}+1}+v_{2j_{r}}v_{2j_{r}+1})\times\\
&\begin{pmatrix}b^{N-j_{g}}v_{2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N}&a^{N-j_{g}}u_{2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{s=1}^{g-1}\begin{pmatrix}0&a^{j_{s+1}-j_{s}}u_{2j_{s}+2\rightarrow 2j_{s+1}-1}\\
b^{j_{s+1}-j_{s}}v_{2j_{s}+2\rightarrow 2j_{s+1}-1}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)\begin{pmatrix}a^{j_{1}}u_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1}\\
b^{j_{1}}v_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1}\end{pmatrix}\\
=&2^{n}\eta^{g}\prod_{r=1}^{g}(u_{2j_{r}}u_{2j_{r}+1}+v_{2j_{r}}v_{2j_{r}+1})(a^{-j_{1}+j_{2}-j_{3}+\cdots}b^{j_{1}-j_{2}+j_{3}-\cdots}u_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1,2j_{3}+2\rightarrow 2j_{4}-1,\cdots}v_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,2j_{2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{3}-1,\cdots}\\
&+a^{j_{1}-j_{2}+j_{3}-\cdots}b^{-j_{1}+j_{2}-j_{3}+\cdots}u_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,2j_{2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{3}-1,\cdots}v_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1,2j_{3}+2\rightarrow 2j_{4}-1,\cdots})\\
=&2^{n}\eta^{g}\prod_{r=1}^{g}(\frac{2^{2\xi}}{v_{2j_{r}}v_{2j_{r}+1}}+v_{2j_{r}}v_{2j_{r}+1})\times\\
&\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &a^{N-j}b^{j}2^{(2N-2j-g)\xi}\frac{v_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,2j_{2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{3}-1,\cdots,2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1}}{v_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1\cdots 2j_{g-2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g-1}-1,2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N}}+\\
&a^{j}b^{N-j}2^{(2j-g)\xi}\frac{v_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1\cdots 2j_{g-2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g-1}-1,2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N}}{v_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,2j_{2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{3}-1,\cdots,2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1}}\end{aligned},&j=\sum_{s=1}^{g}(-1)^{s+1}j_{s},\quad\text{if }\mod(g,2)=1,\\
\begin{aligned} &a^{N-j}b^{j}2^{(2N-2j-g)\xi}\frac{v_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1,2j_{3}+2\rightarrow 2j_{4}-1,\cdots 2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1}}{v_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,\cdots,2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1,2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N}}+\\
&a^{j}b^{N-j}2^{(2j-g)\xi}\frac{v_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,\cdots,2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1,2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N}}{v_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1,2j_{3}+2\rightarrow 2j_{4}-1,\cdots 2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1}}\end{aligned},&j=\sum_{s=1}^{g}(-1)^{s}j_{s},\quad\text{if }\mod(g,2)=0.\end{cases}\\
=&2^{n-t}\eta^{g}\prod_{r=1}^{g}(2^{2\xi}+v^{2}_{2j_{r}}v^{2}_{2j_{r}+1})\times\\
&\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &a^{N-j}b^{j}2^{(2N-2j-g)\xi}v^{2}_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,2j_{2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{3}-1,\cdots,2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1}+\\
&a^{j}b^{N-j}2^{(2j-g)\xi}v^{2}_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1\cdots 2j_{g-2}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g-1}-1,2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N},\end{aligned}&j=\sum_{s=1}^{g}(-1)^{s+1}j_{s},\quad\text{if }\mod(g,2)=1,\\
\begin{aligned} &a^{N-j}b^{j}2^{(2N-2j-g)\xi}v^{2}_{2j_{1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{2}-1,2j_{3}+2\rightarrow 2j_{4}-1,\cdots 2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1}+\\
&a^{j}b^{N-j}2^{(2j-g)\xi}v^{2}_{1\rightarrow 2j_{1}-1,\cdots,2j_{g-1}+2\rightarrow 2j_{g}-1,2j_{g}+2\rightarrow 2N},\end{aligned}&j=\sum_{s=1}^{g}(-1)^{s}j_{s},\quad\text{if }\mod(g,2)=0.\end{cases}\end{split}

(262)

Here,
u_{i_{1}\rightarrow j_{1},\cdots,i_{s}\rightarrow j_{s}}
means
\prod_{r=1}^{s}\prod_{t=i_{r}}^{j_{r}}u_{t}
. The meaning is the same for
v_{i_{1}\rightarrow j_{1},\cdots,i_{s}\rightarrow j_{s}}
. We also use
t=\sum_{i=1}^{2N}n^{i}_{F}
or
2^{t}=\prod_{i=1}^{2N}v_{i}
in the above derivation.

Now, we come back to considering the condition of random erasure noise channels and take the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}
. Note that the erasure channel
\mathcal{N}
only influences the value of
v_{i}
. For a quantity
v_{i_{1}\rightarrow j_{1},\cdots,i_{s}\rightarrow j_{s}}
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}v^{2}_{i_{1}\rightarrow j_{1},\cdots,i_{s}\rightarrow j_{s}}&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}2^{2\sum_{r=1}^{s}\sum_{t=i_{r}}^{j_{r}}{n^{t}_{F}}}.\end{split}

(263)

Denote
X=\sum_{r=1}^{s}\sum_{t=i_{r}}^{j_{r}}{n^{t}_{F}}
. This is a random variable representing the number of erasures in the region
\{i_{1},i_{1}+1\cdots,j_{1},i_{2},i_{2}+1\cdots,j_{2},\cdots,i_{s},i_{s}+1,\cdots,j_{s}\}
. Since we consider the
t
erasures to be equally distributed on all of the qubits, the random variable
X
satisfies

\Pr_{\mathcal{N}}(X=x)=\frac{\binom{m}{x}\binom{n-m}{t-x}}{\binom{n}{t}}=\frac{\binom{t}{x}\binom{n-t}{m-x}}{\binom{n}{m}},

(264)

where
m=\absolutevalue{\{i_1,i_1+1\cdots,j_1,i_2,i_2+1\cdots,j_2,\cdots,i_s,i_s+1,\cdots,j_s\}}\xi
. We prove that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}2^{2X}&=\sum_{x=\max(0,m+t-n)}^{\max(m,t)}2^{2x}\frac{\binom{t}{x}\binom{n-t}{m-x}}{\binom{n}{m}}\\
&\leq(1+\frac{3t}{n})^{m}\\
&\leq 2^{m\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})}.\end{split}

(265)

This can be seen that
\sum_{x=\max(0,m+t-n)}^{\max(m,t)}2^{2x}\binom{t}{x}\binom{n-t}{m-x}
is the coefficient of
z^{m}
in the expansion of the function
(1+4z)^{t}(1+z)^{n-t}
. With Lemma
9
proved later, the coefficients of
z^{m}
of
(1+4z)^{t}(1+z)^{n-t}
is not larger than that of
(1+\frac{4t+n-t}{n}z)^{n}
, of which the coefficient of
z^{m}
is
(1+\frac{3t}{n})^{m}\binom{n}{m}
.

Thus, under the expectation of the location of the erasures, we get

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2N}&v_{2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=j_{g}+1}^{N-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)CE_{2j_{g},2j_{g}+1}\cdots CE_{2j_{2},2j_{2}+1}\left(\prod_{i=j_{1}+1}^{j_{2}-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)CE_{2j_{1},2j_{1}+1}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j_{1}-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)C\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{pmatrix}\\
\leq&2^{n-t}\eta^{g}\sum_{h=0}^{g}\binom{g}{h}(a^{N-j}b^{j}2^{(2N-2j-g+2h)\xi+(2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})}+a^{j}b^{N-j}2^{(2j-g+2h)\xi+(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})})\\
\leq&2^{n-t}2^{-g\xi}\sum_{h=0}^{g}\binom{g}{h}(2^{-2n-\frac{N-j}{N}k}2^{(2N-2j-g+2h)\xi+(2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})}+2^{-2n-\frac{j}{N}k}2^{(2j-g+2h)\xi+(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})})\\
\leq&2^{-t-k}2^{-\frac{g}{2N}n}\sum_{h=0}^{g}\binom{g}{h}(2^{\frac{2j\xi}{n}k}2^{-(2j+g-2h)\xi+(2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})}+2^{(1-\frac{2j\xi}{n})k}2^{-(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi+(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})})\\
=&2^{-t-k}2^{-\frac{g}{2N}(n-k)}\sum_{h=0}^{g}\binom{g}{h}(2^{\frac{(2j-g)\xi}{n}k}2^{-(2j+g-2h)\xi+(2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})}+2^{(1-\frac{(2j+g)\xi}{n})k}2^{-(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi+(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})})\\
=&2^{-t-k}2^{-\frac{g}{2N}(n-k)}\sum_{h=0}^{g}\binom{g}{h}(2^{\frac{(2h-2g)\xi}{n}k}2^{-(2j+g-2h)\xi(1-\frac{k}{n}-\log(1+\frac{3t}{n}))}+2^{\frac{(2h-2g)\xi}{n}k}2^{-(2N-2j+g-2h)\xi(1-\frac{k}{n}-\log(1+\frac{3t}{n}))}).\end{split}

(266)

Note that
j=\sum_{s=1}^{g}(-1)^{s+1}j_{s}
or
j=\sum_{s=1}^{g}(-1)^{s}j_{s}
imply
j\geq\frac{g}{2}
. As long as

1-\frac{k}{n}-\log(1+\frac{3t}{n})\geq 0,

(267)

we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2N}&v_{2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=j_{g}+1}^{N-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)CE_{2j_{g},2j_{g}+1}\cdots CE_{2j_{2},2j_{2}+1}\left(\prod_{i=j_{1}+1}^{j_{2}-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)CE_{2j_{1},2j_{1}+1}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{j_{1}-1}CD_{2i,2i+1}\right)C\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{pmatrix}\\
&\leq 2^{1-t-k}2^{-\frac{g}{2N}(n-k)}\sum_{h=0}^{g}\binom{g}{h}2^{\frac{(2h-2g)\xi}{n}k}\\
&=2^{1-t-k}(2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})^{g}.\end{split}

(268)

Combining Eq. (
261
) and (
268
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\tr\mathbb{E}_{U}[\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})]^{2}\\
&=2^{n}\begin{pmatrix}u_{2N}&v_{2N}\end{pmatrix}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}C(D_{2i,2i+1}+E_{2i,2i+1})\right)C\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{pmatrix}\\
\leq&2^{-t-k}+2^{-n+t}+\sum_{g=1}^{N-1}\binom{N-1}{g}2^{1-t-k}(2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})^{g}\\
=&2^{-t-k}+2^{-n+t}+2^{1-t-k}((1+2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})^{N-1}-1).\end{split}

(269)

Note that
\xi=\log(n/\varepsilon)
and
N=n/2\xi
. Utilizing
(1+x/n)^{n}\leq e^{x}\leq 1+2x
for sufficiently small
x
, we get

\begin{split}(1+2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})^{N-1}-1&\leq 2N(2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})\\
&\leq 4N2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})}\\
&=\frac{2n}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}2^{-\log(n/\varepsilon)(1-\frac{k}{n})}\\
&=\frac{2\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}.\end{split}

(270)

The condition that
x
is small can be achieved by
(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{1-\frac{k}{n}}=o(\frac{1}{n})
.

Integrating Eqs. (
238
), (
242
), and (
269
), we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{U}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}&\leq\left(2^{t+k}(-2^{-t-k}+2^{-t-k}+2^{-n+t}+2^{1-t-k}((1+2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})^{N-1}-1))\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
&=\left(2^{-(n-2t-k)}+2((1+2^{-\xi(1+\frac{k}{n})}+2^{-\xi(1-\frac{k}{n})})^{N-1}-1)\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
&\leq\left(2^{-(n-2t-k)}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

(271)

Proof is done.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Given
a,b>0
,
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, the coefficient of
z^{t}
in the expansion of
(1+az)^{m}(1+bz)^{n}
is not larger than that in the expansion of
(1+(am+bn)z/(m+n))^{m+n}
for any
0\leq t\leq m+n
.

Proof.

We prove Lemma
9
by induction. For any
a,b>0
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, when
n=1
, the coefficient of
z^{t}
is

\binom{m}{t}a^{t}+\binom{m}{t-1}a^{t-1}b,

(272)

for
(1+az)^{m}(1+bz)^{n}
, and is

\binom{m+1}{t}(\frac{ma+b}{m+1})^{t}

(273)

for
(1+(am+bn)z/(m+n))^{m+n}
;
t
takes values from
0
to
m+1
. Now we need to prove

\binom{m}{t}a^{t}+\binom{m}{t-1}a^{t-1}b\leq\binom{m+1}{t}(\frac{ma+b}{m+1})^{t},

(274)

which is equivalent to

1+\frac{t}{m+1}(\frac{b}{a}-1)\leq(1+\frac{1}{m+1}(\frac{b}{a}-1))^{t}.

(275)

Note that
\frac{1}{m+1}(\frac{b}{a}-1)>-1
and when
x>-1
,
(1+x)^{r}\geq 1+rx
for any
r\geq 1
and
r=0
. This can be obtained by considering
g(r)=(1+x)^{r}-(1+rx)
. First,
g(0)=g(1)=0
. Meanwhile, for
r\geq 1
,

\begin{split}g^{\prime}(r)&=(1+x)^{r}\ln(1+x)-x\\
&\geq(1+x)^{r}\frac{x}{1+x}-x\\
&=((1+x)^{r-1}-1)x\\
&\geq 0.\end{split}

(276)

In the second line, we use the inequality
\ln(1+x)\geq x/(1+x)
. Thus, for any
r\geq 1
,
g(r)\geq 0
. As a consequence, Eq. (
275
) is correct, and we prove the case for
n=1
.

Now assuming that for any
a,b>0
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, the claim is correct for
n\leq n_{0}
, we try to prove the claim for
n=n_{0}+1
. First, using the case
(m,n_{0})
, we have

(1+az)^{m}(1+bz)^{n_{0}}\leq(1+\frac{am+bn_{0}}{m+n_{0}}z)^{m+n_{0}}.

(277)

Here,
p_{1}(z)\leq p_{2}(z)
means that each coefficient of
z^{t}
in
p_{1}(z)
is less or equal to that in
p_{2}(z)
. Next, using the case
(m+n_{0},1)
, we have

(1+\frac{am+bn_{0}}{m+n_{0}}z)^{m+n_{0}}(1+bz)\leq(1+\frac{a(m+1)+bn_{0}}{m+1+n_{0}}z)^{m+n_{0}+1}.

(278)

Combining the equations above, we prove the case for
n=n_{0}+1
. The whole proof is done by induction.
∎

B.11
Proof of Proposition
1

In this part, we prove Proposition
1
, which shows that applying random Pauli matrices before encoding and after decoding ensures that the recovery fidelity for any arbitrary input state is lower-bounded by the Choi fidelity.

Proof of Proposition
1
.

Consider the Stinespring dilation of the channel
\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{E}
, which can be represented as an isometry
V_{\mathcal{N}}
from
L
to
SE
. The pure state
V_{\mathcal{N}}\ket{\psi}_{LR}
is a purification of
\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{E}(\psi_{LR})
. A recovery channel
\mathcal{D}
can also be represented as a unitary
U_{\mathcal{D}}
acting on
S
and an ancillary system
A
. By Uhlmann’s Theorem
[
84
,
27
]
, the Choi fidelity can be expressed as:

\begin{split}F_{\mathrm{Choi}}&=\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\absolutevalue{(\bra{\phi}_{LR} \otimes\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE} U_{\cD}V_{\cN} (\ket{\phi}_{LR} \otimes\ket{0}_{A})}\\
&=\frac{1}{d_{L}}\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\absolutevalue{\tr_L[(\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE} U_{\cD}V_{\cN} (I_L\otimes\ket{0}_{A}) ]}.\end{split}

(279)

The recovery fidelity for input state
\psi
using the recovery channel
\mathcal{\mathcal{D}}
can be written as

F_{\psi}=\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\absolutevalue{(\bra{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE} U_{\cD}V_{\cN} (\ket{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\ket{0}_{A})}.

(280)

Now, we evaluate the expected recovery fidelity when applying a random Pauli matrix. The expression for the expected recovery fidelity is given by:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{P_{L}}F_{\psi}&=\mathbb{E}_{P_{L}}\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\absolutevalue{(\bra{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE} P^{\dagger}_L U_{\cD}V_{\cN} P_L (\ket{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\ket{0}_{A})}\\
&\geq\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{L}}\absolutevalue{(\bra{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE}P^{\dagger}_L U_{\cD}V_{\cN} P_L (\ket{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\ket{0}_{A})}\\
&\geq\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\absolutevalue{ \bE_{P_L}(\bra{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE} P^{\dagger}_L U_{\cD}V_{\cN} P_L (\ket{\psi}_{LR} \otimes\ket{0}_{A})}\\
&=\frac{1}{d_{L}}\max_{U^{\prime}_{(S\backslash L),AE}}\absolutevalue{\tr_L[(\bra{0}_{(S\backslash L), AE}) U'_{(S\backslash L), AE} U_{\cD_0}V_{\cN} (I_L\otimes\ket{0}_{A}) ]}\\
&=F_{\mathrm{Choi}}.\end{split}

(281)

In the first line, we apply
P_{L}
before encoding and set
P^{\dagger}_{L}U_{\mathcal{D}}
as the recovery channel. The second line swaps the maximum outside, which reduces the average fidelity. In the third line, we move the expectation inside the absolute value, further reducing the fidelity. Finally, the fourth line applies Pauli twirling. Specifically, for any state
\ket{\psi_{LR}}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{P_{L}}[P_{L}\psi_{LR}P^{\dagger}_{L}]=\tr_{L}(\psi_{LR})\otimes\frac{I_{L}}{d_{L}}.

(282)

∎

B.12
The Choi error of random Clifford encoding

In this part, we introduce the bound of the Choi error of random Clifford encoding. The results can be viewed as a baseline to measure the performance of other encoding methods. Note that the random Clifford encoding is the random stabilizer code
[
19
]
, which is a good code with a constant encoding rate and linear code distance in general.

Recall that the Choi error is bounded by

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{C}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}},

(283)

where
\mathbb{E}_{C}
is the expectation over the
n
-qubit Clifford group. We can apply the non-smooth decoupling theorem for the 2-design group, or Lemma
6
for further evaluation:

\mathbb{E}_{C}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 2^{-\frac{1}{2}H_{2}(S|R)_{\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}}-\frac{1}{2}H_{2}(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})}}.

(284)

Another choice is the smooth decoupling theorem shown in Lemma
6
:

\mathbb{E}_{C}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 2^{-\frac{1}{2}H_{2}(S|R)_{\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}}-\frac{1}{2}H^{\delta}_{\min}(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})}}+8\delta.

(285)

Note that we only smooth
H_{2}(S|E)
with
H^{\delta}_{\min}(S|E)
while maintaining
H_{2}(S|R)
to evaluate a tighter bound like what did in the proof of Corollary
2
. Particularly,

\begin{split}H_{2}(S|R)_{\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}}&=H_{2}(L|R)_{\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}}\\
&=-k.\end{split}

(286)

Below, we continue the evaluation for specific noise models. For the Pauli noise,

\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p})^{\otimes n}

(287)

with
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}
as the complementary channel of the single-qubit Pauli noise
\mathcal{N}_{p}(\rho)=p_{I}\rho+p_{X}X\rho X+p_{Y}Y\rho Y+p_{Z}Z\rho Z
. Particularly,

\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})=(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}))^{\otimes n}.

(288)

When applying the non-smooth decoupling theorem, we first calculate

H_{2}(S|E)_{(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}))^{\otimes n}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})}=n(1-f(\vec{p}))

(289)

by Eq. (
188
). Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq 2^{-\frac{n}{4}(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}.

(290)

Next, we apply the smooth decoupling theorem to Pauli noise. Note that

\frac{1}{n}H^{\delta}_{\mathrm{min}}(S|E)_{\rho^{\otimes n}}\geq H(S|E)_{\rho}-\frac{4\log\gamma\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta^{2}}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(291)

and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{4\log\gamma\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta^{2}}}}{\sqrt{n}}=0.

(292)

For sufficiently large
n
, we have
\frac{4\log\gamma\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta^{2}}}}{\sqrt{n}}<\delta
. Meanwhile,
H(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}=1-h(\vec{p})
. Hence, for any
\delta>0
,

\mathbb{E}_{C}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\leq 2^{-\frac{n}{2}(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}+8\delta;

(293)

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{8\delta+2^{-\frac{n}{2}(1-h(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}}.

(294)

The derivation for the i.i.d. erasure error and local amplitude damping noise is similar. For strength-
p
i.i.d. erasure error, we can get

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq 2^{-\frac{n}{4}(1-\log(1+3p)-\frac{k}{n})}

(295)

with the non-smooth decoupling theorem, and

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{8\delta+2^{-\frac{n}{2}(1-2p-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}}

(296)

with the smooth decoupling theorem.

For strength-
p
local amplitude damping noise, we can get

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq 2^{-\frac{n}{4}(-\log( \frac{1}{2-p} + \sqrt{\frac{p}{2-p}} )-\frac{k}{n})}

(297)

with the non-smooth decoupling theorem, and

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{8\delta+2^{-\frac{n}{2}(h(\frac{1-p}{2})-h(\frac{p}{2})-\frac{k}{n}-\delta)}}

(298)

with the smooth decoupling theorem.

For strength-
p
1D nearest neighbor
ZZ
-coupling noise, the analysis is done by the non-smooth decoupling theorem. The
H_{2}(S|R)
term is still equal to
-k
, and the
H_{2}(S|E)
term is given by

\begin{split}H_{2}(S|E)_{\tau_{SE}}&=-\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}[\tr_{S}\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{zz}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\otimes n})}]\\
&=-\log\tr_{S^{\prime}}[\tr_{S}\prod_{\{i,i+1\}\subseteq[n]}[(1-p)\mathcal{I}+p\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i+1}](\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})]\\
&=n-2\log( (\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})^{n-1})\\
&\geq n[1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})].\end{split}

(299)

Thus, for
ZZ
-coupling noise, we have

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq 2^{-\frac{n}{4}(1-2\log(\sqrt{1-p}+\sqrt{p})-\frac{k}{n})}.

(300)

Finally, we consider the
t
-erasure errors. The random Clifford encoding is robust to a fixed
t
-erasure error rather than a random erasure error. The number of erasure errors should satisfy the Singleton bound
n-2t-k\geq 0

[
40
]
. The corresponding complementary channel is
\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}=\tr_{n-t}
. By applying the non-smooth decoupling theorem, we have that

\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})=\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\otimes t}_{TE}\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{\bar{T}}}{2^{n-t}};

(301)

\begin{split}H_{2}(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S^{\prime}\rightarrow E}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{SS^{\prime}})}&=-\log\tr( ((\frac{\id_{E}}{2^t})^{-\frac{1}{4}}
\ketbra{\hat{\phi}}^{\otimes t}_{TE} \otimes\frac{\id_{\Bar{T}}}{2^{n-t}} (\frac{\id_{E}}{2^t})^{-\frac{1}{4}})^2)\\
&=n-2t.\end{split}

(302)

Here,
T
represents the region of the erasure errors. Combining the results above, we get the upper bound of the Choi error against a fixed
t
-erasure error for the random Clifford encoding:

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq 2^{-\frac{1}{4}(n-2t-k)}.

(303)

B.13
Comparison to the block-encoding scheme

In this part, we compare the double-layer blocked encoding scheme and the block-encoding scheme. We consider two types of noise models. For local Pauli noise, we provide evidence that the double-layer blocked encoding scheme might have an advantage. For local erasure error, we rigorously establish an advantage of the double-layer blocked encoding scheme over the block-encoding scheme in certain error regimes.

Local Pauli noise.
We first consider the local Pauli noise. As mentioned in the main text, the encoding unitary operation of the block-encoding method is

U=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\frac{N}{2}}U_{C}^{4i-3,4i-2,4i-1,4i},

(304)

where
U_{C}^{4i-3,4i-2,4i-1,4i}
is a random Clifford gate on regions
4i-3,4i-2,4i-1,4i
. The set of the random block-encoding unitary operations is denoted as
\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}
.

To compare the performance of the two encoding methods, we only need to evaluate

\mathbb{E}_{U}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}

(305)

under two encoding ensembles
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}
. For simplicity, here we only analyze the results associated with the non-smooth decoupling theorem. The case with the smooth decoupling theorem is similar.

The result of
\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}
is given by Eq. (
190
). The result of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}
can be established by considering the error of each block and the triangle inequality. Specifically,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\frac{n}{4\xi}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{4\xi}^{B}}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{\frac{4\xi(n-k)}{n}}}{0^{\frac{4\xi(n-k)}{n}}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{4\xi}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{\frac{4\xi k}{n}}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\frac{n}{4\xi}2^{-\frac{1}{2}H_{2}(L|R)_{\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}^{\otimes 4\xi k/n}}-\frac{1}{2}H_{2}(S|E)_{(\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}))^{\otimes 4\xi}}}.\end{split}

(306)

Using
H_{2}(S|E)_{\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{p}(\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}})}=1-f(\vec{p})
, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\frac{n}{4\xi}(2^{-2\xi(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})})\\
=&\frac{n}{4\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{2(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}.\end{split}

(307)

Recall that the result of the double-layer blocked encoding is given by

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}}\|\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(U\outerproduct{\hat{\phi}}{\hat{\phi}}_{LR}\otimes\outerproduct{0^{n-k}}{0^{n-k}}U^{\dagger})-\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{S\rightarrow E}(\frac{\mathbb{I}_{S}}{2^{n}})\otimes\frac{\mathbb{I}_{R}}{2^{k}}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2^{-n(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}+\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}.\end{split}

(308)

For block encoding, the dependence of error on
n
is given by
\frac{n}{4\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{2(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}
. For double-layer blocked encoding, it is approximately given by two terms
\sqrt{2^{-n(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}}
and
\sqrt{\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}
. Note that
\varepsilon
is normally chosen to make
\frac{\varepsilon}{n}=1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
. Meanwhile,
1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}
is often a constant. Thus, an exponentially decay term must vanish faster:

\sqrt{2^{-n(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}}\ll\frac{n}{4\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{2(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}.

(309)

Also, when
1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n}<\frac{1}{4}(1-\frac{k}{n})
, we have

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}}{\frac{n}{4\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{2(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}}=8\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}{n}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{2f(\vec{p})-\frac{3}{2}(1-\frac{k}{n})}=0.

(310)

Thus,

\sqrt{\frac{4\varepsilon^{1-\frac{k}{n}}n^{\frac{k}{n}}}{\log(n/\varepsilon)}}\ll\frac{n}{4\log\frac{n}{\varepsilon}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{n})^{2(1-f(\vec{p})-\frac{k}{n})}.

(311)

That is, given the noise strength
\vec{p}
, when the encoding rate satisfies
\frac{k}{n}>1-\frac{4}{3}f(\vec{p})
, the error given by the double-layer blocked encoding decays much faster. In other words, if we fix a required Choi error decay scaling, the encoding rate offered by the double-layer blocked scheme can be higher. This provides evidence that the double-layer blocked encoding scheme can have an advantage in some error regimes. Nonetheless, it is a comparison of the upper bound of the error. Below, we consider local erasure errors and evaluate a lower bound of the Choi error given by the block-encoding method. This establishes a rigorous advantage of the double-layer blocked encoding scheme.

Local Erasure error.
To establish the lower bound of the Choi error given by the block-encoding method, we only need to evaluate the lower bound of the Choi error of each block, which corresponds to a random stabilizer encoding.

For a random stabilizer code
[[n,k]]
, correctability under erasure error requires the number of erasure errors
t
to satisfy the singleton bound:

2t+k\leq n\quad\Rightarrow\quad t\leq\left\lfloor\frac{n-k}{2}\right\rfloor.

(312)

Define
\tau=\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{k}{n})
. Then any erasure pattern erasing more than
\tau n
qubits is uncorrectable. Moreover, as long as the number of erasure errors is
t>\left\lfloor\frac{n-k}{2}\right\rfloor
, we can prove that there exists a lower bound of the Choi error for random stabilizer codes. The result is summarized as follows.

Theorem 6
.

Let
t\leq n/2
. Suppose
Q
is a subset of
n
qubits with
\absolutevalue{Q}=t
. Then the expectation of Choi error against an erasure error on
Q
over
[[n,k]]
random stabilizer codes has a lower bound:

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{Q}\geq\frac{2t-(n-k)-e^{-1}}{8k},

(313)

where
\epsilon_{Q}
is the Choi error
\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}
for erasure on
Q
.

We postpone the proof of Theorem
6
to the end of this subsection. With the number of erasure errors increasing, the Choi error also monotonically increases. Thus, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 9
.

For
t>n/2
,

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{Q}\geq\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{Q^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{ek},

(314)

where
\absolutevalue{Q'}=n/2
.

For strength-
p
local erasure errors, each qubit is erased independently with probability
p
. The number of erased qubits satisfies a binomial distribution
B(n,p)
, where we denote this number or random variable as
E_{n}
. Based on Theorem
6
, the Choi error is always lower bounded by
\frac{1-e^{-1}}{8k}\geq\frac{1}{16n}
when
E_{n}>\tau n
. Then the Choi error of a random stabilizer code is lower bounded by
\frac{1}{16n}\Pr\left[E_{n}>\tau n\right]
. Using Chernoff bounds, for
\tau>p
or
1-\frac{k}{n}-2p>0
, we have

\Pr\left[E_{n}>\tau n\right]=\exp\left(-n\cdot D\left(\tau\,\|\,p\right)+o(n)\right),

(315)

where
D(a\|b)
is the binary relative entropy:

D(a\|b)=a\log_{2}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)+(1-a)\log_{2}\left(\frac{1-a}{1-b}\right).

(316)

Thus, the Choi error satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{C}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\geq\frac{1}{16n}\exp\left(-n\cdot D\left(\tau\Big\|p\right)+o(n)\right)=\exp\left(-nD\left(\tau\Big\|p\right)+o(n)\right).

(317)

To further give a lower bound of the Choi error for the block-encoding scheme, we introduce the near-optimal fidelity
[
104
]
, denoted as
\widetilde{F}_{\mathrm{Choi}}
, with the corresponding near-optimal Choi error denoted as
\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{Choi}}
. Compared to the definition of original Choi fidelity,

F_{\mathrm{Choi}}=\max_{\mathcal{D}}F\left(\hat{\phi}_{LR},[(\mathcal{D}\circ\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{E})_{L}\otimes I_{R}](\hat{\phi}_{LR})\right),

(318)

the near-optimal fidelity uses the transpose channel recovery for decoding,

\widetilde{F}_{\mathrm{Choi}}=F\left(\hat{\phi}_{LR},[(\mathcal{D}^{TC}\circ\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{E})_{L}\otimes I_{R}](\hat{\phi}_{LR})\right).

(319)

The transpose channel recovery
\mathcal{D}^{TC}
is defined as
[
104
]

\mathcal{D}^{TC}=P_{L}\circ\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(P_{L}).

(320)

Here,
\mathcal{N}
is the noise channel. The map
P_{L}
satisfies
P_{L}(\rho)=\Pi_{L}\rho\Pi_{L}
, where
\Pi_{L}
is the projector to the logical space. The map
\mathcal{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(P_{L})
satisfies
[\mathcal{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(P_{L})](\rho)=\mathcal{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Pi_{L})\rho\mathcal{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Pi_{L})
.

It has been proved that there exists two-sided bounds between the optimal fidelity and the near-optimal fidelity
[
105
,
104
]
:

\frac{1}{2}(1-\widetilde{F}_{\mathrm{Choi}})\leq 1-F_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq 1-\widetilde{F}_{\mathrm{Choi}}.

(321)

Using the relation
F=\sqrt{1-\epsilon^{2}}
, we have that for sufficiently small
\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}
,

\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}.

(322)

The left inequality follows from the near-optimality. The right inequality is derived by

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}(1-\sqrt{1-\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}})\leq 1-\sqrt{1-\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}}\\
\Leftrightarrow&4(1-\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}})\leq 2-\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}+2\sqrt{1-\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}}\leq 2-\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}+2(1-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}})\\
\Leftrightarrow&\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{Choi}}\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}.\end{split}

(323)

Note that in the second line we use
\sqrt{1-x}\leq 1-\frac{1}{2}x
for sufficiently small
x
.

From the definition of the transpose recovery map, it can be seen that for a block-encoding scheme
\mathcal{E}=\bigotimes_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}
and block noise model
\mathcal{N}=\bigotimes_{i}\mathcal{N}_{i}
, the transpose recovery map is also a tensor product,
\mathcal{D}^{TC}=\bigotimes_{i}\mathcal{D}^{TC}_{i}
. Thus, given a block-encoding scheme, since each block is independent, and the noise also acts independently on each block, the near-optimal fidelity for the whole block-encoding scheme is the product of the near-optimal fidelity for each block.

\widetilde{F}_{\mathrm{Choi}}=\prod_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{4\xi}}\widetilde{F}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}},

(324)

or equivalently,

\widetilde{F}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}=\prod_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{4\xi}}\widetilde{F}^{2}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}.

(325)

Then,

1-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{Choi}}^{2}=\prod_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{4\xi}}(1-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}^{2}),

(326)

where

\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}\geq\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\epsilon_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}\geq\exp\left(-4\xi\cdot D\left(\tau\Big\|p\right)+o(\xi)\right).

(327)

Thus, we obtain the lower bound of the Choi error for the block-encoding scheme:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\epsilon_{\mathrm{Choi}}&\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{Choi}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}_{\mathrm{Choi}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}(1-\prod_{i}(1-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}^{2}))\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1-\prod_{i}(1-\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}^{2}))\\
&\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1-\prod_{i}(1-[\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathfrak{S}_{n}^{B}}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{block}_{i}}]^{2}))\\
&\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1-(1-\exp\left(-8\xi\cdot D\left(\tau\Big\|p\right)+o(\xi)\right))^{\frac{n}{4\xi}})\\
&\geq\min\left\{\frac{n}{8\sqrt{2}\xi}\exp\left(-8\xi\cdot D\left(\tau\Big\|p\right)+o(\xi)\right),\Omega(1)\right\}\\
&=\min\left\{n\exp\left(-8\xi D\left(\tau\Big\|p\right)+o(\xi)\right),\Omega(1)\right\}.\end{split}

(328)

Here, the fourth and fifth lines use the independence of each block and
\mathbb{E}x^{2}\geq(\mathbb{E}x)^{2}
, respectively. The last inequality comes as follows. Let
m\coloneqq\frac{n}{4\xi}
and
x\coloneqq m\exp(-8\xi\cdot D\left( \tau\Big\| p \right) + o(\xi))
. Then
